## Document 018: 2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2014年GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ ～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026年 11月 6日

1/7 
2014年 GPIF改革から考える国内債券の位置づけ 
～「金利のある世界」で現行配分は最適か～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  奥田 宏二

（要旨） 
○ GPIFが基本ポートフォリオを変更し、国内債券の割合を引き上げるのではないかとの観測が
市場で浮き沈みしている。本稿では、現在の基本ポートフォリオの起点となった2014年の運
用改革の成立過程を分析し、変更する場合にどのようなことが起き得るのか考察する。 
○ 2014年改革では、基本ポートフォリオに占める国内債券を60％から35％へ大幅に引き下げ、
国内外の株式を約 2倍に増やした。アベノミクスの象徴の一つとして GPIF改革は語られる
ことが多いが、改革につながる議論は民主党政権期から始まっていた。 
○ 当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事録によれば、株式をどこまで増やすかより、国内債券をどこま
で減らすかが議論の中心だった。また、当時決定した国内債券35％という配分は、期待収益
率やリスクだけで機械的に決まったものではなく、移行コストや市場への影響などを考慮し
て決定された。市場への影響を抑えるため、正式決定前から乖離許容幅を使って国内債券を
減らしていたのは、今後の動向を見極めるうえで興味深い。当面、四半期の運用実績に注目
が集まるだろう。 
○ 足元では長期金利が上昇し、低金利が懸念点だった2014年当時とは逆の環境にある。当時の
運用委員会では、金利が上がれば国内債券の期待収益率が高まり、最適化をやり直すことで
国債の割合が上昇し得るとの説明がなされていた。当時の考え方を踏まえれば、現在の基本
ポートフォリオが「金利のある世界」でも最適なのか再考の余地があろう。

### Page 2

2/7 
1. はじめに 
年金積立金管理運用独立行政法人（GPIF）を巡り、基本ポートフォリオの見直し観測が市場で
浮き沈みしている。きっかけは片山さつき財務・金融担当相が2026年7月、GPIFをはじめとする
年金基金について、日本の金融資産への投資をさらに増やす方向を後押ししたいとの考えを示し
たことだ。長期金利が上昇するなかでの発言で、日本の金融資産とは特に国内債券だと解されて
いる。こうしたなか、GPIF側では同年 8月の経営委員会の議事次第に「基本ポートフォリオ検証
等PTにおける議論について」が報告事項に入り、経営委員会への関心が高まった。 
GPIFの基本ポートフォリオは5年に1度、公的年金の財政検証を踏まえて見直す。現在の基本
ポートフォリオは2025年度が開始年だ。制度上、毎年度検証し、必要があれば5年間の中期目標
期間中でも見直しを検討することになっているものの、GPIF発足後、中期目標期間の途中で基本
ポートフォリオを変更したのは2013年 6月と 2014年 10月の 2回にとどまる。 
2014年10月の見直しでは、国内債券比率を60％から 35％へ引き下げ、国内外の株式をそれぞ
れ12％から25％へ引き上げる大幅な変更が行われた。株式比率の上昇によって損失が大きくなる
リスクを懸念する向きはあったが、運用損失だけでなく、年金財政上、目標とする利回りを達成
できないこともリスクと捉える考え方を明確にした。2014年改革の本質は公的年金運用における
「安全」という概念を変えたことだと筆者はみている。 
では、仮にいま国内債券を再び増やすとなれば、改革の逆行を意味するのか。この問いを考え
るため、年金財政からの議論と成長戦略からの要請という二つの流れを民主党政権期からたど
り、2014年改革がどのように実現したのかを振り返る。

2. 民主党政権期――国債中心の「安全運用」への疑問 
2014年改革は第2次安倍晋三政権の経済政策「アベノミクス」の象徴の一つとして語られるこ
とが多い。しかし、その成立過程をさかのぼると、改革につながる議論は第2次安倍政権の発足
前からあった。民主党政権期の議論では、公的年金の運用における「安全」を巡る議論が次第に
成熟していったことが確認できる。 
2006年に発足したGPIFの基本ポートフォリオは、当初国内債券が67％を占めていた（資料1）。
このうち、大半を占める国債は満期に額面で償還される。国債偏重の狙いは、厚生労働省の検討
会が 2010年12月にまとめた報告書にも表れている。公的年金運用の考え方として、保険料は
「投資を目的として徴収されたもの」ではないとして、 「安全運用が基本」とした（注1） 。検討会
では、全額を国債で運用すべきだとの意見もあった。

### Page 3

3/7 
資料 1 GPIFの基本ポートフォリオの変遷

（注）2014年 10月以降は短期資産を基本ポートフォリオに含めない。 
（出所）GPIFの公表資料より第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

もっとも、国債を多く持つことが本当に安全なのかという疑問はすでにこの検討会で示されて
いた。運用上のリスクを単なる損失だけで捉えず、年金財政上予定された収益を下回り、将来の
保険料率の引き上げや給付の引き下げにつながる可能性まで考慮する必要があるとしている。国
債を多く持てば安全だという単純な整理には収まらない議論が存在していたことが確認できる。 
この検討会に続く社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会では、国債への集中はリスクになると
いう考えが強く示されるようになる。2012年 2月に同委員会の議論を整理した資料（注2）では
国債のみの運用は一つの資産への集中であり、分散投資ができていないという意味でリスクが非
常に大きい、という指摘が確認できる。デフレと低金利がこの先も続くとは考えにくく、物価や
賃金が上昇すれば、給付する年金額は増える一方で債券価格は下落し、損失を被る可能性が高い
というアベノミクス下での改革を先取りするような意見も出た。国債のみの運用で、将来にわた
り物価や賃金の上昇率を上回る利回りを確保できるのか疑問が残る、との意見もあった。 
民主党政権後期の議論は、短期の運用損失を過度に重視するのではなく、国債偏重によって年
金財政上、必要な利回りを確保できないリスクを考慮する方向へと発展していった。

3. 産業競争力会議――成長戦略からの見直し要請 
GPIF改革に至る過程では、日本経済の成長戦略の文脈で国債偏重の見直しを求める意見があっ
た。これは安倍政権時だけでなく民主党政権期にもあった。国内債券を減らすべきだという方向
は、年金財政からの議論と重なる。ただ、見直しを求める理由は両政権期とも公的年金の巨額の
資金を経済成長に生かすという発想から来ている。 
安倍政権でGPIF改革の舞台となったのは、政権発足直後の2013年 1月に始まった産業競争力会
議だ。 「公的・準公的資金の有効活用」が初回から議論にのぼり、安全資産に偏った年金などの運
用を見直し、成長分野への資金供給につなげる考え方が示された。同会議が進むなかでは、民間

### Page 4

4/7 
議員から「アクティブ運用をし、そこに中長期でもの言う株主ということで企業が新陳代謝も進
めるようなツールとしていく」といった発言が飛び出している（注3）。GPIFの資金を企業改革や
経済成長に生かそうとする考え方であり、 「専ら被保険者の利益」のために運用するという年金積
立金の原則との緊張をはらんでいた。

4. 有識者会議――成長戦略と被保険者利益の両立 
成長戦略のなかで出たこうした要請を、公的年金の運用原則とどのように両立させたのだろう
か。この問題に対応したのが「公的・準公的資金の運用・リスク管理等の高度化等に関する有識
者会議」だった。この会議は政府の日本再興戦略を受けて経済再生担当相の下に設置され、GPIF
改革の理論的柱を打ち立てた。 
公的年金の運用に国民が何を最も期待しているのか明確に定義した点がこの会議のポイントだ
ろう。2013年 11月の報告書は、 「現役世代の保険料負担を維持しつつ、将来の年金給付に支障が
生じないよう、年金財政の長期的な健全性の確保に資すること」こそが国民の最も期待している
ことだとした（注4）。目標とする運用利回りが達成できなければ、保険料の引き上げか将来の年
金給付水準の引き下げが迫られる可能性が生じる。 
この定義に則り、デフレから脱却して適度なインフレ環境へ移るというアベノミクスの目指し
た環境でのポートフォリオのあり方を検討した。安全資産とされる国債を多く持てば安全だと考
えるのではなく、年金財政に必要な収益を確保できるかという観点から基本ポートフォリオを考
える方向が明確に示された。 
収益率の向上という文脈のなかで、産業競争力会議で出たようなGPIFの資金を企業改革に生か
すというアイデアは運用改革に取り込まれていく。投資先企業との対話や議決権行使を通じて企
業価値の向上を促し、その成果を運用収益として享受するという考え方が報告書で示された。成
長戦略が期待した企業改革を、被保険者の利益につながる運用につなげたのだ。 
社会保障審議会年金部会の専門委員会もこれに呼応する形で、デフレから脱却して適度なイン
フレ環境へ移る状況では、あらかじめ「国内債券中心」と示す必要はなく、GPIFが運用目的・目
標に沿ったポートフォリオを検討すべきだとした。年金積立金の運用目標は、年金給付費が長期
的には名目賃金上昇率に連動することから、賃金上昇率をどの程度上回るかで設定される。同専
門委員会は、財政検証に向けて検討した複数の経済前提を踏まえ、GPIFが確保すべき運用利回り
として、名目賃金上昇率を1.7％上回る水準を示した（注5）。

5. GPIF運用委員会――国内債券 35％の決定過程 
ここから2014年 10月の基本ポートフォリオ変更に至る議論を、当時のGPIF運用委員会の議事
録からたどる。2014年改革は、株式を増やした改革とみられがちだ。しかし、運用委員会の議論
の中心になったのは株式の比率ではなく、国内債券の扱いだった。後年公表された議事録を読む
と、国内債券中心では目標の利回りが達成できないどころか、金利が上昇した場合には多額の損
失を被るといった懸念が、当時の事務局や理事長から示されている。当時の長期金利は0.5％前後

### Page 5

5/7 
と、歴史的な低水準にあった（資料2）。事務局の試算も、国内債券の比率を 45％以下に減らさな
ければ目標の利回りに届かない、という形で示された。

資料 2 日本国債 10年物利回り

（注）10年物国債利回りの月末値。2026年 11月は 6日の値。 
（出所）Bloombergより第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

GPIF内部の議論では株式をどこまで増やすかではなく、国内債券をどこまで減らすかが議論の
焦点だったことが分かる。運用委員会の委員長は最終段階で、 「国債を減らしたというのが一番の
主で、その結果として、こういうポートフォリオが書けた」と振り返っているのが象徴的だろう
（注 6）。 
基本ポートフォリオの決定に至る議論で興味深いのは、GPIFの売買が国内市場に与える影響を
相当程度考慮に入れていた点だ。事務局から示された基本ポートフォリオ候補は当初、五つあっ
た。このなかには、現在のポートフォリオである4資産を25％ずつとする案も含まれていた。こ
の案は、現状からの移行を考えると「現実感がゼロ」とされ、早い段階で検証の対象から外れた
（注 7）。事務局は候補を絞り込む際に足元の資産構成からの距離を物差しに加え、最終案の選定
理由にも「足下の資産構成と相対的に近く、移行に係るコストが小さい」ことを挙げている（注
8）。最終段階では、1％刻みで確認すると、経済中位ケースでは国内債券34％などのより効率的な
配分も見つかった。それでも、国内債券をさらに減らして国内株式を増やせば移行の影響が大き
くなるとして、35％が維持された。外国市場に比べ、国内市場ではGPIFの売買の影響が大きいと
いう判断が働いていた。 
国内債券の売却は、正式な決定を待たずに始まっていた。運用委員会は2014年4月の時点で、
現行の基本ポートフォリオが認める範囲の中で国内債券を減らす方針を了承している。委員長は

### Page 6

6/7 
その目的を、金利上昇に備えて「債券の評価損を抑える」ことだと確認した（注9）。8月には、
その範囲を超えて国内債券を売り、ほかの資産に振り向ける方針も了承された。事務局は、積立
金規模が巨額であるため、市場インパクトやフロントランニングを誘発せずに速やかに資産配分
を変更することは現実的ではないと説明し、基本ポートフォリオの正式決定前から資産配分の変
更を検討する考えを示した（注 10）。正式決定に先立つ8月末には、国内債券の比率はすでに
50％程度まで下がっていた。 
GPIFの現在の中期目標でも、市場の価格形成や民間の投資行動等を歪めないことが求められて
いる。今後、基本ポートフォリオを変更する場合にも、市場への影響を抑えるため、正式な変更
に先立って資産配分を調整することが考えられる。今回も運用の動向を見極めるうえで、四半期
の運用実績が注目される。

6. おわりに 
2014年にGPIFが中期目標期間の途中で基本ポートフォリオを変更した背景には、先行きの運用
環境の変化を見通した判断があった。GPIFは当時、日本経済が「長年続いたデフレからの転換と
いう大きな運用環境の変化の節目」にあるとし、 「長期的な経済環境の変化に速やかに対応する観
点から、来年度を待たず」に変更に踏み切った（注11） 。実際には低金利がその後も長く続いた
が、足元では当時懸念されていた金利上昇が現実のものとなっている。 
長期金利は2026年 9月に3％台に達し、約30年ぶりの水準を付けた。奇しくも3％という数字
は、2014年のGPIF運用委員会でもたびたび登場していた。当時の理事長は、国内債券60％という
従来の基本ポートフォリオについて、 「国内債券の利回りが3％というものを大前提としてつくっ
ている」と述べていた（注12）。 
興味深いのは、2014年の運用委員会で、金利が上昇すれば国内債券の将来の期待収益率が高ま
り、改めて資産配分の最適化を行うことで「自然に国債の割合がまたもとに戻るというか、上が
っていく」とGPIFの事務局側が説明していたことだ（注13）。2020年の基本ポートフォリオ変更
では、国内金利の低下を踏まえ、国内債券を減らして外国債券を増やした。今後は、長期の収益
確保を担う株式の比率を維持しつつ、為替リスクなどを考慮して外国債券の一部を国内債券に振
り向けることも選択肢として考えられる。2014年に国内債券を減らしたのと同じ考え方が、今度
はその割合を引き上げる判断につながり得るのではないだろうか。

## Document 035: 20261027【投研報告】市場快訊_MSCI降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待

Source PDF: all report pdf/20261027【投研報告】市場快訊_MSCI降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (市場評論)
Date: 2026-10-27
Pages: 3

MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market） 」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定 。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至 「邊境市場 （Frontier Market
） 」 ，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低
觸及 4,907.37 點 。 雖然20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈 ， 但26 日再度下跌 1.22%

，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」， 原則上將不再納入MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外 。 相關改革有助改

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續 ， 以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證 ， 我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異 ， 區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響 ， 以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

公司簡介 
台達電為全球最大交換式電源供應器廠商 ，
為因應全球產業競爭加劇 ， 已逐步從關鍵零
組件製造商邁入整體節能解決方案提供者 ，
3Q26 營收占比：電源及零組件 (Power 
Electronics) 54% 、 基 礎 設 施
(Infrastructure) 33% 、 自 動 化
(Automation) 8%、交通部門  (Mobility) 
5%。近年 PC 產業已漸趨飽和，台達電積
極切入資料中心系統解決方案、工廠自動
化 、 樓宇自動化以及電動車及儲能應用 ， 由
傳統電源製造轉型至節能相關領域 ， 並藉由
自動化及被動元件布局提升生產效率 ， 有效
帶動獲利攀升。

張智皓 chihhao.chang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：法說會

台達電 (2308 TT) 
成長趨勢未變

永豐觀點 
3Q26 受到資料中心相關產品物料狀況不順影響，獲利不及預期，然預估4Q26 出
貨將轉順，獲利將回歸成長。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：雖近期受到料況影響獲利狀況，然台達電受惠 AI 資料中心建置需求未
變，電源及散熱零組件規格不斷提升，台達電獲利將受惠於規格持續提升，營運能
見度佳，訂單持續累積。另一方面，台達電在電力佈局持續，SOFC 解決方案將於
2027 年開始出貨。評價方面，台達電為電源及液冷產品龍頭廠商市占率高，受惠
程度明顯，營運將進入成長期，故以2027 年稅後 EPS 56.88 元計算，目前本益比
約 26 倍，處於歷史評價區間下緣，考量台達電逐漸從過往的零組件供應商轉型成
為系統整合商的角色，有利於在產品市占及獲利能力的提升，且獲利成長性顯著，
故維持買進建議，目標價 2130 元(30X 2027-2028 平均 EPS)。

ESG 評析 
台達電企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向
平均得分分別為 B+等與 A+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

台達電為全球 Power 龍頭廠商：台達電為全球最大交換式電源供應器廠商，為因
應全球產業競爭加劇，已逐步從關鍵零組件製造商邁入整體節能解決方案提供者，
3Q26 營 收 占 比 ： 電 源 及 零 組 件(Power Electronics)54% 、 基 礎 設 施
(Infrastructure)33%、 自動化(Automation)8%、交通部門(Mobility)5%。 近年PC
產業已漸趨飽和，台達電積極切入資料中心系統解決方案、工廠自動化、樓宇自動
化以及電動車及儲能應用， 由傳統電源製造轉型至節能相關領域， 並藉由自動化及
被動元件布局提升生產效率，有效帶動獲利攀升。主要競爭對手為光寶科及國外電
源供應器廠商與電源相關零組件供應商。 
資料中心相關業務仍為成長主軸 ：電源及零組件部門(Power Electronics)部門營收
1059.3 億(+10% QoQ，+40% YoY)，成長主要來自資料中心電源及液冷出貨，營
業利益 199.2 億(-1% QoQ，+22% YoY)，營利率 18.8%；交通(Mobility) 部門營
收 90.7 億(+10% QoQ，+7% YoY)，受電動車需求持續疲弱，營業損失 1.37 億；
自動化(Automation)部門營收 159 億(+2% QoQ，+19% YoY)，因物料轉順，營
業利益 7.69 億元 ， 營利率4.8%； 基礎設施(Infrastructure)部門營收 658.8 億(+5% 
QoQ，+25% YoY)，成長來自資料中心液冷產品持續出貨，營業利益 120.68 億，
營利率 18.3%。整體來說，台達電主要動能來自於高階伺服器電源及散熱相關產
品，在自動化略有復甦，交通部門虧損收斂。 
供應鏈料況及資料中心建置遞延影響，3Q26 獲利不及預期：台達電 3Q26 營收
1970.59 億元(+7.5%QoQ，+31.1%YoY)，因長短料影響毛利率約 1.7%，毛利率
34.1%(-1.5ptsQoQ，-0.8ptsYoY)， 不及預期的36%， 營利率16.6%(-0.1ptsQoQ，
+0.1ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 234.95 億元(-6.5%QoQ，+26.3%YoY)，稅後 EPS 9.05
元，低於預期的 9.98 元。 
4Q26 電源及散熱產品出貨暢旺：受惠於 AI 伺服器需求持續持續增加，先前遞延
訂單將出貨，研究部上修預估 4Q26 營收 2162.3 億元(+9.7%QoQ，+33.8%YoY)，
在價格調漲及部分庫存迴轉，毛利率35.5%(+1.4pts QoQ，+0.9ptsYoY)，營利率
18.5%(+1.9pts QoQ ，+2.2ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 296.47 億元 (+26.2%QoQ，
+71.1%YoY)， 稅後EPS 為 11.41 元 。 預估2026 年營收 7588.99 億元(+36.2%YoY)，
毛利率 35.5%(+1.2ptsYoY)，營利率 17.4%(+2.3ptsYoY)，稅後淨利 988.33 億元
(+64.4%YoY)，稅後 EPS 為 38.05 元。 
AI 零組件產品內容價值提升及規模效應下，台達電成長快速：展望未來，電源產
品方面，隨著伺服器對電源需求增加，Vera Rubin 單顆 PSU 18.3kW 產品已經出
貨。此外，正負 400V DC 電源產品已小量出貨，預估將於 2H27 量產，成為未來

King Yuan Electronics Co (2449.TW)
06 November 2026 Citi Research
3
Near-term weakness already in the price; maintain Buy and lower TP to NT$286
- Despite near-term margin pressure from softer-than-expected quarterly revenue
and rising depreciation cost, electricity and OPEX, management sees GM
expansion into next year supported by stronger utilization, operating leverage, and
favorable AI-related product mix, in particular substantial upside from wafer

sorting. We lower our earnings projection for 2026/2027 /2028 by 6%/17%/15%
reflecting near-term GM shortfall and potential increasing depreciation cost.
Considering remaining strong AI cycle, we keep our Buy rating but lower our DCF-
derived TP to NT$286 given our lower estimates and concerns on recent
macro/policy uncertainty. Our new TP of NT$286 implies 25x/17x of our 2027 /28
EPS estimates.

Bull/Bear: King Yuan Electronics Co (2449.TW)

### Page 6

King Yuan Electronics Co (2449.TW)
06 November 2026 Citi Research
6
King Yuan Electronics Co
Company description
Founded in 1987, King Yuan Electronics Corp. (KYEC) mainly provides testing
services for the back-end process of semiconductor production globally. With
more than 30 years of industry experience, KYEC has built up firm
relationships with several leading global IC design/IDM customers. Its
headquarters and production facilities are both located in Taiwan, with sales

offices in North America, Japan, and Singapore.
Investment strategy
We rate KYEC as Buy. As a key testing service provider for AI GPU and ASIC
products, KYEC is well positioned to benefit from the significant AI growth
opportunity in the years to come. Currently, KYEC is the major final testing
service provider for its major customer's AI GPU, and we expect KYEC to
continue to secure its dominant share in its customer's upcoming AI GPU
product lines due to its specialty in burn-in testing, smooth product delivery

capability, and its strong business partnership with its customer. We are also
positive over the growth outlook of its smartphone business thanks to the
integration of AI functionality, leading to a better replacement cycle. We
believe KYEC's major customer in smartphone will benefit from an improved
product mix, which will result in longer testing times for the flagship SoC.
Valuation
Our target price for KYEC of NT$286 is derived using a DCF methodology.

Using Bloomberg data, we apply a risk-free rate of 4%, a risk premium of 5%,
and a beta of 1.4x for a 10.8% cost of equity. With 20% corporate tax and a
3.0% interest rate on its debt, our WACC for KYEC is 9.7%. We assume
terminal sales growth of 5%. We model its adjusted EBIT margin to gradually
improve from 23% in 2024 to c30% in 2035E. At our target price, the shares
would trade at 2027 /28E P/B of 4x/4x and 2027 /28E P/E of 25x/17x, in line
with its global peers.

Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent KYEC shares from reaching our target
price include: 1) weaker-than-expected end-demand for global
smartphone/consumer electronic products; 2) slower ramp for AI-related
products; 3) slower-than-expected demand recovery in the automotive and
base station sector; and 4) pricing pressure on increasing competition.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

Wiwynn

AI-driven sales growth since 4Q; reiterate 
Buy and trim PO to NT$2,310

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 2,310 TWD | Price: 1,555 TWD

3Q beat driven by both general and ASIC servers 
Wiwynn’s 3Q sales grew 56% QoQ/63% YoY, supported by sustained general server 
demand as well as a strong ramp of ASIC servers (e.g., AWS Trainium 3) from Sep. 
GM/OPM of 8.87%/7.47% was above BofAe of 7.77%/6.42% and consensus of 
8.42%/6.85%. OP was 40%/16% above BofAe/consensus. After a non-op. loss (mainly 
from higher interest expenses), NP was 34%/10% above BofAe/consensus.  
4Q sales led by ASIC; GPU sales expected by year-end 
The company posted strong Oct sales of NT$165.1bn (-4% MoM/+81% YoY), tracking 
35%/36% of BofA/consensus estimates. Its general server business was impacted by 
component shortages in Oct, despite demand remaining strong. For AI servers, we 
expect ASIC to be the major sales driver in 4Q with a strong ramp since Sep; while GPU 
servers (from multiple customers) are expected to deliver sales contribution by year-end.  
Solid sales growth in 2027-28; GM pressured by AI  
Our checks indicate that Wiwynn’s ASIC projects remain on a buy-and-sell model, 
although a consignment business model is under discussion; while business models for 
GPU projects have yet to be confirmed. With strong AI server momentum, we expect its 
GM to decline QoQ to 7.67% in 4Q26. For 2027/28, we model solid YoY sales growth of 
+60%/+31% , with conservative GMs of 6.67%/6.51% in 2027/28, vs. 8.30% in 2026E.  
Reiterate Buy; trim PO to NT$2,310 
We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 4-14%, mainly to reflect the 3Q26 beat and solid sales 
growth aided by general and ASIC server projects. We however trim PO to NT$2,310 
(15x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS) from NT$2,387 (18x 2H26-1H27E EPS), as we roll over our 
valuation base and lower multiple on macro uncertainties and concerns over margin 
decline. Reit. Buy on robust ASIC momentum and a constructive general server outlook.

Key Metrics      
* For full definitions of iQmethod SM measures, see page 7.

Company Sector 
IT Hardware

Company Description 
Wiwynn supplies server, storage and cloud equipment to 
global cloud service providers (CSPs). 100% of its sales 
comes from server/datacenter business. Key clients are 
Meta, Microsoft, and Amazon. Wiwynn was founded in April 
2012 and was listed on Taiwan Stock Exchange on 27 
March 2019. As of 2Q26, Wistron (3231 TT) owned 35.27% 
of shares in Wiwynn. Besides the headquarter in Taiwan, its 
subsidiaries and operations are located in Malaysia, Hong 
Kong, China, South Korea, Japan, the US and Mexico.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Wiwynn as we are positive on 
increasing contribution from AI server projects, delivery in 
Amazon after efforts for years, and regular server 
bottoming out from 2H23, driving double-digit 
sales/earnings growth in 2026-28E.

### Page 5

Price objective basis & risk 
Wiwynn (XWIQF) 
Our PO of NT$2,310 is based on 15x 4Q26-3Q27E EPS, which is 1.5-2.0SD above 
Wiwynn's historical P/E average since listing. We believe such a multiple is reasonable to 
reflect Wiwynn's rising sales contribution from AI server, delivery in Amazon projects, 
and regular server demand bottoming out from 2H23.

Upside risks to our PO are: (1) higher-than-expected capex growth from key clients, (2) 
stronger-than-expected momentum in server/datacenter infrastructure, (3) more project 
wins/launches for AI server, and (4) much higher contribution from the third largest 
customer.

Downside risks to our PO are: (1) unexpected slowdown of key clients' capex spending in 
server/datacenter, (2) more intensified competition from peers, and (3) weaker 
momentum in AI server projects.

iQmethod SMis the set of BofA Global Research standard measures that serve to maintain global consistency under three broad headings: Business Performance, Quality of Earnings, and validations. The key features of 
iQmethod are: A consistently structured, detailed, and transparent methodology. Guidelines to maximize the effectiveness of the comparative valuation process, and to identify some common pitfalls. 
iQdatabase ®  is our real-time global research database that is sourced directly from our equity analysts’ earnings models and includes forecasted as well as historical data for income statements, balance sheets, and cash 
flow statements for companies covered by BofA Global Research.

of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment 
rating of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, 
the 0-12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a 
stock should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

股價相對大盤走勢

晶豪科 (3006 TT)  Trading Buy

報價收斂，後續成長動能受限，建議 Trading Buy 。  
投資建議 ：AI 伺服器對 HBM 多層晶圓堆疊的強勁需求大幅吸
納三大原廠先進 DRAM 產能 ， 引發傳統記憶體供給端嚴重缺口 ，
缺貨潮由 DDR5 與 DDR4 蔓延至 DDR3 、 DDR2 及 NOR/NAND 
Flash 。同業大廠如華邦電 (2344 TT) 基於晶圓廠經濟效益考量，
產能優先配置予大容量 DDR4 產品，對 DDR2 與 DDR3 採取停
供或減供措施，大幅限縮市場流通供給。目前晶豪科 DDR2 與
DDR3 合計佔 DRAM 營收比重超過 50% ，在同業減供退出的趨
勢下，晶豪科已成為市場核心主要供應商，市占地位結構性擴
張。預估晶豪科 2027 年稅後 EPS 127.72 元。雖然記憶體價格
在 2027 年仍可以維持高檔，但目前報價漲幅開始收斂，產能供
給受限，成本持續上揚，後續成長動能受限，因此對晶豪科投
資建議為 Trading Buy 。  
3Q26 稅後 EPS 36.09 元： 受惠國際大廠把產能轉至 HBM、DDR5
等高階產品時，成熟型 DRAM、DDR2/DDR3 等利基型記憶體供
給減少，帶動報價上揚，晶豪科 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元，
QoQ+58.83% ，毛利率增加 8.75 個百分點至 77.44% ，3Q26 稅後
淨利 107.59 億元，QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元。1Q26~3Q26
營收 433.63 億元，年增 3.439 倍，毛利率 69.62% ，較 2025 年
同期增 60.08 個百分點，歸屬母公司淨利 187.3 億元，稅後 EPS 
64.54 元。  
預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 39.41 元： 展望 4Q26 營運表現，儘管整體
出貨數量受限於上游晶圓代工與封測端產能配額難見大幅擴
增，但由於 4Q26 產品平均銷售單價 (ASP) 延續上漲趨勢，漲幅
約 10~20% 。但晶圓代工從 07/2026 漲約 45% ，封測約漲 20% ，
將會逐步反映在 4Q26 銷貨成本，經營團隊預期 4Q26 毛利率將
穩健維持在 3Q26 之歷史高檔區間，來自產品售價調升幅度足
以抵銷成本增幅。預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 39.41 元。預估 2026 年
稅後 EPS 102.25 元。

2 群益投顧 
  晶豪科 ESG ：  
企業永續與治理架構  
• 企業永續委員會：於 10/2020 成立，負責評估永續經營的風險與機會、制
訂永續發展願景與策略。  
• 核心推動構面：涵蓋強化公司治理、夥伴關係、環境永續、友善職場及社
會公益。  
• 永續報告書：定期依循國際標準 (如  GRI 、 TCFD 、 SASB 等 )編製與揭露
年度永續報告書 ， 可至晶豪科技 ESG 報告書或耀智永續報告書資料庫  查閱
與下載。  
環境與氣候行動  
• 減碳目標：積極因應全球氣候變遷，設定具體減碳目標，其減碳表現經評
估朝向升溫  1.5°C 以下的目標邁進 (實測減碳溫度約  1.506°C) 。  
• 永續金融連結：曾與金融機構 (如台新銀行、北富銀等 )合作推動永續績效
聯貸案 (30 億元聯貸案 )，將  ESG 指標與授信條件結合，落實綠色金融與企
業社會責任。  
社會責任與人才培育  
• 社會公益與教育：設立「晶豪科技教育基金會」，長期投入教育與社會公
益相關推廣。  
• 人才與實習計畫：積極與青年世代接軌，辦理暑期研發與  ESG 實習生計
畫，深化校園人才交流與職場體驗。  
記憶體價格調漲，晶豪科 3Q26 營收 221.50 億元， QoQ+58.83% ，稅後淨利
107.59 億元， QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元：  
晶豪科為國內利基型記憶體  IC 設計公司，產品涵蓋利基型 DRAM 產品組
合，涵蓋從傳統 SDRAM 到主流的 DDR 、 DDR2 、 DDR3 及 DDR4 。廣泛應
用於消費性電子、工業控制、網通設備、 PC 週邊，公司主要是 4Gb 以下為
主。在 Flash 方面，晶豪科有 SLC NAND Flash 以及編碼型快閃記憶體 (NOR 
Flash) 。因應中低階智慧手機、系統級封裝 (SiP) 及固態硬碟 (SSD) 需求快速
成長，公司以 MCP/eMCP/eMMC 為主力產品。類比和數位的產品
(Audio/Power/Motor Driver/Sensor) 佔公司營收比重低。各產品佔比， DRAM
佔營收比重最高，約 60~65% ， Flash 和其他約佔 30~33%(MCP 等也有含
DRAM) ，非記憶體佔營收比重 5%。  
受惠國際大廠把產能轉至 HBM 、 DDR5 等高階產品時，成熟型 DRAM 、
DDR2/DDR3 等利基型記憶體供給減少，帶動報價上揚，晶豪科 3Q26 營收
221.50 億元， QoQ+58.83% ，毛利率增加 8.75 個百分點至 77.44% ， 3Q26 稅
後淨利 107.59 億元， QoQ+82.12% ，稅後 EPS 36.09 元。  
累計 1Q26~3Q26 營收 433.63 億元，年增 3.439 倍，毛利率 69.62% ，較 2025
年同期增 60.08 個百分點，歸屬母公司淨利 187.3 億元，稅後 EPS 64.54 元。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 3 日

3 群益投顧 
3Q26 底晶豪科的存貨為 90.76 億元，庫存水準較 2Q26 的 79.56 億元，
QoQ+14.08% ，要反映晶圓廠與封測調價造成的成本墊高，而非量的顯著增
加。  
合作晶圓廠現階段增產幅度相對受限，預估 4Q26 營收 243.02 億元，
QoQ+9.72% ，稅後淨利 117.49 億元， QoQ+9.20% ，稅後 EPS 39.41 元：  
晶圓代工端產能配置方面，晶豪科 DRAM 主要晶圓代工廠為力積電 (6770 
TT) ，也是力積電記憶體最大的代工客戶。主要代工夥伴力積電因先前將部
分廠房出售予 Micron(MU US) ，現有 12 吋晶圓廠中 P1 與 P2 廠原生產邏輯
代工，在 3Q26 調整至生產記憶體代工，但整體而言，記憶體代工產能釋出
受限，爭取晶圓代工與後段封測產能 (Wafer & Backend) 成為晶豪科當前最
關鍵的營運課題。  
力積電於 07/2026 全面啟動新一波代工報價調漲，其中針對 DRAM 投片價
格大幅調升約 45% 。考量自晶圓投入、前段製造至出貨之生產週期 (Cycle 
time) 約需 5~6 個月，晶豪科配合客戶訂單展開拉貨後，該批高價晶圓成本
將於 4Q26 正式反映於銷貨成本 (COGS) 之中。主要封測代工夥伴南茂 (8150 
TT) 亦自 07/2026 起調漲 20% 之封測費用，受限於約 6 個月之封測與成品週
轉週期，亦遞延至 11/2026 正式認列於營業成本。  
然而，由於市場端面臨華邦電等同業對成熟製程 DDR2 與 DDR3 減供或停
供的結構性缺口，終端訂單滿足率目前僅達 60% 至 70%，晶豪科具備極為強
勁的賣方定價話語權。在 4Q26 產品 ASP 持續走高，且漲幅 10~20% 的帶動
下 ， 晶豪科有能力將晶圓與封測端增加的成本全數轉嫁予客戶端 ， 支撐 4Q26
毛利率穩健維持在 3Q26 之 77.44% 歷史高檔區間。預估晶豪科 4Q26 營收
243.02 億元， QoQ+9.72% ，稅後淨利 117.49 億元， QoQ+9.20% ，稅後 EPS 
39.41 元。  
預估晶豪科 2027 年營收 898.37 億元， YoY+32.77% ，稅後獲利 380.72 億
元， YoY+24.91% ，稅後 EPS 127.72 元：  
晶豪科的產品組合，記憶體佔營收比重最高，約 95%。記憶體當中， DRAM
佔 65% 左右，以 KGD 為主。其他則為 NAND 、 NOR 以及其他
MCP/eMCP/eMMC 等。 DRAM 當中， DDR2 和 DDR3 佔目前 DRAM 出貨量
50% 。  
市場競爭格局方面， AI 伺服器對 HBM 多層晶圓堆疊的強勁需求大幅吸納
三大原廠先進 DRAM 產能，引發傳統記憶體供給端嚴重缺口，缺貨潮由
DDR5 與 DDR4 蔓延至 DDR3 、 DDR2 及 NOR/NAND Flash 。同業大廠如華
邦電基於晶圓廠經濟效益考量 ， 產能優先配置予大容量 DDR4 產品 ， 對DDR2
與 DDR3 採取停供或減供措施，其中華邦電之 DDR2 僅保留供應特定策略
夥伴，大幅限縮市場流通供給。目前晶豪科 DDR2 與 DDR3 合計營收比重
超過 50%，在同業減供退出的趨勢下，晶豪科已成為市場核心主要供應商，
市占地位結構性擴張。  
終端需求方面， AI PC、筆記型電腦與智慧型手機單機搭載記憶體容量提升，
加上實體 AI( 如人形機器人與自駕載具 )逐步落地，帶動非 HBM 記憶體需求
持穩增長。消費性電子端雖因記憶體 ASP 漲幅較大出現部分觀望情緒，惟
晶豪科高階與商務應用拉貨依然暢旺，客戶訂單滿足率受限於產能瓶頸，整

### Page 4

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 11 月 3 日

4 群益投顧 
體營運動能無虞。展望 2027 年營運，管理層指出全球傳統記憶體產能供給
增長極為有限，供需失衡格局短期難以扭轉，預期市場供不應求狀態將延續
至 2027 年全年，支撐產品報價結構性維持堅挺， 2027 年整體營運維持樂觀
基調。預估晶豪科 2027 年營收 898.37 億元， YoY+32.77% ，稅後獲利 380.72
億元， YoY+24.91% ，稅後 EPS 127.72 元。  
股利政策方面，管理層指出將經董事會討論定案，過往配發率曾達 50%，預
估未來現金股利發放率將落在 30% 至 50% 區間。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

## Document 267: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지HD현대중공업(329180) 업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지업종 내 여전히 매력적인 선택지 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-15

### Page 1

HD현대중공업의 3Q26 연결 실적은 매출액 5조 8,602억원(yoy +33%, qoq -7%), 
영업이익 9,161억원(yoy +64%, qoq -12%, OPM 15.6%)으로 시장 컨센서스를 하회할 
전망이다. 동사의 3Q26 조업일수는 여름휴가, 추석연휴 및 중대재해 등 영향으로 
qoq -21% 감소했으며 환율 역시 qoq -84원 감소한 영향을 감안해 전분기 대비 
실적을 보수적으로 추정했다. 그럼에도 해양 부문 Trion FPU발 C/O에 따른 일회성 
환입이 예상됨에 따라 일부 이익 상쇄가 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 한편, 동사의 
임단협 부결로 당분기 추가적인 인건비 부담은 제한적일 것으로 판단되며 잠수함 
화재 사고 관련 비용 역시 4Q 이후 반영될 전망이다.

동사는 급격히 커져가는 AIDC향 전력수요 및 발전용 엔진 수주에 대응하고자 9/10 
총 4GW 규모의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 증설을 위한 신규 투자를 공시헸다. 동사의 
기존 DC향 4행정 중속엔진 수주 단가를 고려 시 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 
가능할 전망이며 2행정 및 선박용 4행정 엔진 대비 높은 수익성이 예상되는 바 
생산이 본격화될 28년부터 동사 실적 성장성의 핵심 동인으로 작용할 전망이다.

동사에 대해 투자의견 BUY를 유지하며 업종 밸류에이션 디레이팅 및 시장 프리미엄 
조정에 따른 Target Multipe 변동으로 목표주가를 70만원으로 하향 조정한다. 상선 
시장 수주 경쟁 심화로 업종 밸류 조정이 이루어지고 있으나 동사는 엔진 기반 장기 
성장성으로 Peer 대비 상대적 매력도 확보 및 주가 방어가 가능하다는 판단이다.

## Document 281: AI“沉船”风险会拖垮美元吗？

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI“沉船”风险会拖垮美元吗？.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-09
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

巴克莱认为，尽管 AI 相关股票遭遇抛售，但美国经济数据持续强劲、 AI 资本开⽀周期短期
内⼤幅下修的可能性有限，美元情绪指标仍处于正值区间，因此倾向于看好逢低买⼊美
元。
摘要：
AI 投资热潮引发的市场动荡，尚未动摇美元的基本⾯⽀撑。据追⻛交易台消息，巴克莱最新外汇
策略报告指出，美国经济数据持续强劲， AI 资本开⽀周期短期内⼤幅下修的可能性有限，这使得
该⾏倾向于看好在美元回调时逢低买⼊策略。
当前美元⻛险溢价已降⾄约 1% ，为逾⼀年来最低⽔平。分析师指出，欧元兑美元或因欧元空头
仓位拥挤⽽短暂升⾄ 1.14 附近，但鉴于美国宏观基本⾯的持续韧性，这⼀⾛势 “ 难以持续 ” 。
AI 资本开⽀的不确定性，正在以不同⽅式影响各主要货币 —— ⽇元因避险需求受益，欧元则因法
国财政⻛险雪上加霜
从 “ 沉船 ” 说起：历史的镜⼦
巴克莱在报告开篇援引了⼀段历史典故： 1858 年，⼯程师 Isambard Kingdom Brunel 主导建造了
当时世界上最⼤的远洋客轮 “ ⼤东⽅号 ” （ SS Great Eastern ）。这艘船耗尽了委托⽅的资⾦，⼏经

易⼿，最终以废铁价格出售，拆解公司本⾝也陷⼊延误和劳资纠纷。
当然，⼤东⽅号的故事并⾮纯粹的悲剧。这艘船后来被⽤于铺设英美之间的海底电报电缆，催
⽣了⼀项⾜以永久改变世界的技术创新 —— 英镑兑美元汇率的俗称 “cable” ，正是由此⽽来。
该⾏以此类⽐当下的 AI 投资浪潮，指出 “ 这或许是⾃ 1990 年代末以来，第⼀次有⼀个如此规模、
如此雄⼼的项⽬ —— 在商业⽤途、盈利能⼒和影响后果上都是未知之旅 —— 正在我们眼前展
开。⽽且，它正在以⼀种超越我们这⼀⽣对 ' ⻛险承担 ' 感知的⽅式演进。 ”
AI 资本开⽀：⻛险在哪⾥？
该⾏直接点出了这轮 AI 资本开⽀周期中最令⼈担忧的结构性问题。
第⼀，需求的真实性存疑。 当前对算⼒的需求，是否被⼀些中期内难以为继的项⽬推⾄虚⾼？
第⼆，交叉融资的传导⻛险。 芯⽚代⼯⼚、 AI 软件公司与超⼤规模云⼚商之间的延伸交叉融
资，是否在单⼀环节失败时会引发多⽶诺⻣牌效应？
第三，数据中⼼建设的政治阻⼒。 美国中期选举结果出炉后，市场也在追问：数据中⼼建设引

发的反弹，是否会造成延误并推⾼成本？这⼜将如何传导⾄芯⽚制造商的估值？
但报告指出，现有数据中⼼建设合同将按计划推进，未来⼏个季度的建设进度不太可能受到实
质性冲击。从最新财报来看，更充裕的算⼒供给正在推动营收增⻓ —— 这在⼀定程度上必须由
企业端的真实需求驱动，⽽⾮仅靠⼤语⾔模型训练。
超⼤规模云⼚商⽬前没有显⽰出放缓借贷投资计划的迹象，实际上他们预计将在明年底前实现正
向现⾦流。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

更重要的是，巴克莱给出了⼀个宏观层⾯的底线判断：“ 这项新技术已经到来，不会离开。我们
⽆论如何都不可能回到两年前的状态。 ”
美元： AI 抛售尚未形成实质冲击
巴克莱的结论是：⾄少⽬前， AI 相关的股票抛售尚未动摇美元的吸引⼒。
报告指出，现有数据中⼼建设合同将按计划推进，未来⼏个季度的资本开⽀不太可能⼤幅缩
减。超⼤规模云服务商⽬前没有放缓 “ 借钱投资 ” 计划的迹象，并预计将于明年底前实现正向现⾦
流。此外，更⾼的算⼒供给正在推动营收增⻓，这在⼀定程度上反映了企业端的真实需求，⽽
⾮仅仅是⼤型语⾔模型训练所驱动。
从宏观层⾯看，美国经济私⼈国内购买者最终销售增速接近 5% ，经济运⾏在潜在产出⽔平之
上，通胀率仍黏在 3% 附近。该⾏认为， “ 资本开⽀周期将会延续，未来⼀年内⼤幅下修的迹象寥
寥⽆⼏。 ”
当前美元情绪指标仍处于略微正值区间，尚未出现净空头信号。该⾏表⽰，市场可能会出现 “ 美
国即将放缓 ” 的叙事，并推动欧元兑美元短暂升⾄ 1.14 左右，但 “ 鉴于美国宏观背景的持续韧性，

⽆论是当前还是前瞻性来看，这⼀⾛势都难以持续。 ”
欧元：反美元吸引⼒持续消退
欧元作为 “ 反美元 ” 主要替代货币的吸引⼒，正在加速褪⾊。
巴克莱指出，欧元的颓势在美联储 9 ⽉加息后已初现端倪，⽽法国 OAT 利差的急剧扩⼤则进⼀步
加重了压⼒。
在货币政策层⾯， AI 相关股票抛售后，市场对欧洲央⾏的终端利率预期已修正⾄更为现实的约
3% ⽔平。但该⾏认为，欧元的货币政策困境在于：鉴于总需求动能的丧失以及法国债券市场的
脆弱性，进⼀步⼤幅加息⾄ 3% 以上的可能性不⼤。
⽇元：避险需求提供⽀撑
法国的财政问题本⾝已⾜够棘⼿，⽽即将到来的总统⼤选和悬峙议会，使得凝聚共识⼏乎成为不
可能完成的任务。
中期前景依然偏负⾯，⽬前唯⼀的近期亮点是情绪⾯仍偏空，存在⼀定的技术性反弹空间。

### Page 3

AI 投资放缓的担忧，通过股票⻛险溢价的扩⼤，为⽇元带来了避险资⾦流⼊。油价下跌同样在原
则上利好⽇元，尽管这⼀效应可能更多体现在⽇元兑其他货币的交叉盘上，⽽⾮兑美元。
该⾏给出了具体的情景测算：若标普 500 指数从当前约 6% 的跌幅进⼀步扩⼤⾄ 10% （即 2023 年
以来的平均回调幅度），美元兑⽇元可能下⾏约 1.5% ，跌向 155 附近；若跌幅扩⼤⾄ 20% ，⽇元
可能升值 4% ⾄ 5% 。
不过，该⾏也提⽰，⾃夏季协调⼲预以来，投机性仓位已整体转为⽇元净多头，这对⽇元进⼀
步升值的速度构成制约。
亚洲其他货币：科技股波动分化影响
AI 及科技股的动荡，正在亚洲货币市场制造明显分化。
韩元（ KRW ）同样受到科技股波动拖累，本⽉外资股票净流出约 59 亿美元，另有约 20 亿美元的
散⼾净流出。巴克莱认为美元兑韩元近期存在上⾏⻛险， 1325 为⽀撑位。
印度卢⽐（ INR ）则相对受益：油价⼤跌、印度股票指数中 AI 敞⼝较低，叠加外资股票流⼊改善
（⽉内净流⼊约 24 亿美元），为卢⽐提供了有利环境。但巴克莱对印度央⾏（ RBI ）远期账⼾规
模扩⼤、外汇储备下降保持警惕，预计美元兑卢⽐不会⻓期维持在 200 ⽇均线 94.22 下⽅。
⻢来西亚林吉特（ MYR ）则⾯临双重压⼒：作为该地区唯⼀的能源净出⼝国，油价下跌对其相
对更为不利；同时，数据中⼼投资担忧也可能拖累⻢来西亚作为亚洲 AI 及数据中⼼枢纽的吸引
⼒。

## Document 284: AI基建融资新裂痕！一批数据中心贷款被打包甩卖，星际之门项目也在其中

荷兰国际集团 ING 正寻求⼀次性抛售约 5 亿美元数据中⼼贷款组合，其中包括与 OpenAI 、
软银、甲⻣⽂联合推进的 " 星际之⻔ "AI 旗舰项⽬相关融资。更令市场警觉的是，甲⻣⽂已向

摘要：
荷兰国际集团 ING 正寻求⼀次性出售约 5 亿美元的数据中⼼贷款组合，其中包括与 OpenAI 、软银
及甲⻣⽂联合推进的 " 星际之⻔ " （ Stargate ） AI 基础设施项⽬相关的融资，此举令本已承压的 AI
基建融资市场再添变数。
据彭博周六报道， ING 通过竞价出售流程（ bids-wanted-in-competition ）挂牌约 12 笔贷款，出
价截⽌⽇期为 11 ⽉ 19 ⽇，单笔出价⻔槛不低于 1000 万美元。
此次抛售发⽣在美国中期选举⺠主党⼤获全胜之后 —— 市场普遍担忧新政治格局将带来更严格

的科技监管，芯⽚股及新兴云计算公司遭到重创，标普 500 成分股市值合计蒸发逾 3 万亿美元。
此次出售的贷款组合中，⼀笔 2500 万美元的融资尤为引⼈关注 —— 该笔贷款与新墨西哥州⼀处
⼤型数据中⼼挂钩，该项⽬正是星际之⻔计划的重要组成部分。甲⻣⽂已就该新墨西哥州项⽬
向开发商发出不可抗⼒通知，意味着甲⻣⽂正在为可能退出相关义务预留法律空间。
星际之⻔项⽬现裂痕，不可抗⼒通知引发警觉

通知。不可抗⼒条款通常是合同⼀⽅为摆脱既有承诺⽽启动的法律程序，意味着该⽅正在主
张 " 不可控事件 " 可免除其合同义务。
星际之⻔是美国规模最⼤的 AI 基础设施旗舰项⽬之⼀，由 OpenAI 、软银及甲⻣⽂联合推进。甲

AI 基建融资⽣态的信⼼构成直接冲击。

养⽼基⾦等追求稳定收益的机构投资者。此次 5 亿美元的⼤规模集中抛售，且卖家选择匿名操
作，将直接检验在政治环境⽣变、股市⼤幅回调的双重压⼒下，这⼀市场的真实承接能⼒。
AI 基建股全线承压，融资成本上⾏⻛险浮现
此次贷款出售对权益市场的传导效应已有所显现。甲⻣⽂作为被直接点名的当事⽅，与英伟
达、微软、 CoreWeave ，以及数据中⼼地产运营商 Digital Realty 和 Equinix ⼀同承压。数据中⼼
运营商 Applied Digital 、 Cipher Digital 及 IREN 本周跌幅均超过 14% 。

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for
every nuance. Lately, there seems to have been more RBA
communication to pore over. The method of delivery has
also evolved. This has sometimes caused confusion when
tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different

tone and varying complexity.
• The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly
using speeches directed to professional audiences to
discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such
as neutral rates and financial conditions. They are also
highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more
ways than just squeezing mortgage holders.
• One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises about the future

path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running
commentary about how it is interpreting recent data. These
interpretations are themselves useful signals about what
the RBA thinks it might do.
Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks
for clues to future rate moves. They will have plenty to pore over
in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry
of fireside chats this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded

interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday. Next
Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before
Senate Estimates, just as the quarterly CPI is released. (This
timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After
the decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a
media release and press conference. More speeches will follow,
including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just

following the RBA Review but also more recently. There may
be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication
outside that certainly feels more frequent than a couple of years
ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little sparse
outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary
appearances. We have counted 58 public engagements so
far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website.
This compares with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in

2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events
were related to economics, markets and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed
as well. There are fewer set-piece speeches on policy and a
Open mouth operations
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few
years ago, there are more interviews across a wide range of

media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It was
a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the
7:30 Report ; it is hard to imagine him agreeing to an Assistant
Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general
audience. Central banks want to explain why they do what
they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising
but the household sector is not the source of the inflation.

Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes
confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief Communication Officer
highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting
media conference is designed to speak to the public. Market
participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
the primary audience. So, if you think that what the Governor
said in the media conference sounded more ‘dovish’ than
what another official says in a more market-oriented forum,

it is partly because she is trying to convey that the RBA
understands that its recent policy actions are making things
harder for many Australians. But this is in much the same way
that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It does, but that
will not stop the treatment.
A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted
material such as the minutes and set-piece speeches than in
unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be

careful with their words in unscripted settings. If the tone
differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have
reduced the pressure to make the set-piece speeches more
accessible. Research on central bank communication often
uses measures such as the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and
Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was

beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison
suggests four speeches from 2026 ( 1, 2, 3, 4) were harder
to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches
scored in the 11 to 12 range, which is high-school-level
“The RBA does not want to promise what it will
do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks. "

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and
15, which is academic or technically dense text requiring
university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-
Governor Phil Lowe also scored better on these metrics than

the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that
readability metrics do not necessarily imply better clarity if the
concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has
evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has begun to put
more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those
invisible ‘star’ variables (NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial
conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences

has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on
the full range of transmission channels from interest rates to
inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour
supply channel). This is understandable in a world of supply
shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt
they would be comforted, though, by the Deputy Governor’s

explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the
smallest of the transmission channels from interest rates
to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on
inflation. It raises the question of whether other policy tools
or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on
inflation through the other channels without hitting a minority
of households disproportionately.

We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases
to summarise the position. From ‘narrow path’ to ‘not
ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once
entrenched, a phrase can outlast its usefulness.
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record
the presentations to the Board and the members’ discussion. The
minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-
meeting statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the

Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The August 2024 minutes
were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how
sensitive the forecasts were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed
Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s
communication have not changed. It wants to explain – and
perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how
it interprets incoming data, especially inflation, the labour

market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business
of providing ‘forward guidance’ about future moves.
Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular
future path for interest rates. Some academic literature argues
that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high,
for that matter) independently amplifies the transmission
of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-period

interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield
curve. Central banks including the RBA have certainly tried
jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view,
though, is that the potential cost of reputational loss if things
do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a
slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides
the signal about future policy directions. This is why it was

noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour
market as ‘a little tight’ after the September meeting, even
though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We
have been noting for some time that the RBA staff have a
downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the RBA’s
oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy
Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not surprise us.

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: managing expectations
While annual retail sales growth improved in September,
rising from 0.4%yr to 1.1%yr, the current pace of growth
is only a quarter of the gain seen just a year ago and very
weak versus history. Consumer spending continues to be

constrained by weak confidence, particularly with respect to
employment prospects and wealth. On housing wealth, five
years into the property downturn, house prices continue to
decline month-on-month and investment remains in a deep
downturn, –20.6%ytd.
Total fixed asset investment has been detracting from
annual GDP growth since April owing to a lull in high-tech
investment, a lack of confidence in future demand amongst
domestically oriented businesses as well as property’s

malaise. For aggregate growth to hold up through 2027 and
2028 as the contribution from net exports moderates, broad-
based stimulus in scale will prove necessary. Expect recent
targeted support measures to be broadened and given much
greater scale in coming months.
Finally, in the UK, inflation accelerated from 3.1%yr to 3.5%yr
in September, exceeding both market expectations and the
Bank of England's July forecast. The surprise was largely
driven by energy, fuel prices rising 5.4%mth. However, core

inflation also edged up from 2.6%yr to 2.8%yr as services
inflation increased from 3.4%yr to 3.7%yr. While not an
immediate concern inflation, the labour market showing
resilience, the ILO unemployment rate falling from 4.9% to
4.7% in August, suggests demand risks for inflation will need
close monitoring as well as supply-side concerns.
Next week will see the focus once again return to the US as
the FOMC meet and US Q3 GDP is released. For both events,

the implications for risks to the outlook will be the focus. The
week after, the US mid-term elections are due.
With little domestic data of significance, participants’
attention focused on RBA Deputy Governor Hauser's fireside
chat, which saw an abrupt shift in market expectations of a
follow-up rate hike at the November meeting.
Hauser warned in his remarks that the current inflation outlook
was "materially worse" than the RBA's August forecasts.

Three upside inflation risks were characterised as having
"crystallised" ahead of the September meeting – the Middle
East, the AI boom, and domestic supply constraints. Hauser
noted the Middle East picture "looks worse" given oil prices
are "about a third higher than [the RBA] assumed in August”.
He then positioned AI and data centre developments among
an "extraordinary sequence of positive demand shocks around
the world", with "higher AI prices, tech prices feeding through

into CPI". Finally, on domestic capacity pressures, the Deputy
Governor noted that "inflation's been coming in at or a little
bit above expectations, despite an apparent easing in the
labour market". He went on to state that the staff are currently
assessing "whether [the RBA] need to change [its] judgement
on capacity pressures in the labour market again" – in other
words, revise up their assumption for the NAIRU.
Hauser's comments prompted a material reassessment of

market pricing for a follow-up November hike, from just a
20% chance at the end of last week to a 50/50 proposition
today ahead of next week's Q3 CPI. For that release, we are
forecasting a 1.3% (4.1%yr) lift in headline inflation and a
0.9 (3.5%yr) increase in trimmed mean inflation. Our preview
provides more detail behind the component mix for Q3 as
well as our upward revisions to the preliminary Q4 forecast.
Provided inflation prints broadly in line with our expectations,

the RBA is likely to hike by 25bp at the November meeting.
Across in New Zealand, consumer prices rose 0.7% in Q3
and annual inflation eased from 4.1%yr to 3.7%yr. Under
the surface of this ‘as expected’ read though, several closely
watched measures of core inflation took a step higher to now
be at or slightly above the top of the RBNZ’s target band. Our
NZ economics team consequently expect the RBNZ will raise
the OCR 25bps to 3.00% at its October policy review, while

retaining a 4.00% terminal OCR forecast for September 2027.
Earlier in the week, China's economy surprised  to the upside in
Q3, GDP growth accelerating from 0.9%qtr to 1.2%qtr, lifting
annual growth from 4.3%yr to 4.5%yr – keeping authorities on
track to achieve their 4.5–5.0% full-year growth objective.
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist
“With little domestic data of significance,
participants’ attention focused on RBA Deputy

Governor Hauser's fireside chat. Offshore,
China GDP was the key release. "

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
RBNZ now expected to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ will deliver a 25bp hike at its
policy review on 28 October. While September quarter
inflation was slightly below the RBNZ’s forecast, the
underlying detail was firm. More recently, increases in

fuel prices point to a material lift in inflation by year-end.
We expect further interest rate increases over the coming
months, with the OCR reaching a peak of 4% next year.
Softer headline inflation but firm core pressures
leave RBNZ on course to hike in October
We now expect the RBNZ to raise the Official Cash Rate
by 25bp at its October policy review (previously, we had
expected a pause at this meeting). This change in view reflects
significant upside risks to near-term inflation relative to the

RBNZ’s previous forecast.
While in line with our forecast, the September quarter inflation
figures were slightly softer than the RBNZ expected, with prices
rising 3.7% over the past year (compared with the RBNZ’s
forecast of 3.9%). However, the details were less reassuring.
Excluding volatile items such as food and fuel, underlying
inflation pressures remained firm. Closely watched measures
of core inflation moved higher and now sit close to, or a little

above, the top of the RBNZ’s target band. We also saw an
increase in the number of goods recording price rises, taking
that measure back to levels seen in the wake of the pandemic.
Alongside the existing firmness in underlying inflation, the
risks for inflation over the coming quarters are firmly tilted to
the upside. That near-term upside risk has also been noted by
the Governor. Following a sharp rise in global fuel costs, we
expect inflation to reach 4.3% by the end of this year (above

the RBNZ’s September MPS forecast for +3.9%). While that is
mainly due to higher prices at the pump, businesses in sectors
such as construction are also reporting related increases in
transport and materials costs. Consistent with those trends,
we expect core inflation to remain in the upper part of the
RBNZ’s target band through 2027.
Reinforcing the case for an OCR hike at the October meeting,
the New Zealand dollar is notably weaker than the RBNZ

assumed: on a trade-weighted basis, the NZD is currently
3.6% below the RBNZ’s September projections. If sustained,
that would add to inflation pressures over the next few
quarters. We suspect this will not have shifted the RBNZ’s
medium-term outlook for inflation.
Several other developments need to be weighed against that
firmer inflation outlook. First, wholesale long-term interest rates
have risen markedly, and there has also been a more moderate

rise in some mortgage rates. The RBNZ is likely to note the role of
those increases in managing medium-term inflation risks.
In addition, the RBNZ is likely to continue to emphasise that
there is little evidence of a lift in businesses’ longer-term plans
for price setting at this stage in response to the rise in energy
prices (sometimes referred to as “second-round inflation
effects”). We expect the RBNZ to highlight the risk that more
persistent inflation pressures could emerge, depending on the

duration of the oil price shock.
We also expect the RBNZ to note that the labour market
remains cyclically weak and is not a significant driver of inflation.
Employment growth that remains weak relative to labour force
growth, subdued growth in job vacancies and low labour market
turnover are consistent with this view and could be emphasised.
Finally, the RBNZ is likely to acknowledge that while the
global growth outlook remains solid, there are significant

downside risks from the Iran war and concerns about the
sustainability of the AI investment cycle.
Balancing those concerns, we think the MPC will lift the
cash rate next week. However, a vote seems very possible as
differences in view will likely remain among the MPC about
how much the medium-term inflation outlook has really
shifted in recent weeks.
In September, the bulk of the MPC saw upside risks to the
inflation outlook while two members (Conway and Hansen)

thought the risks were balanced. Recent developments will
not have made those seeing upside risks to inflation more
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
comfortable, while the more dovish MPC members will
likely focus on the ongoing downside risks for growth given
continued softness in house prices and the mounting pressure
on real incomes. Indeed, all MPC members saw some degree
of downside risks to growth which likely remain relevant. All

these messages should remain in the RBNZ’s updated views.
The MPC is likely to continue to present its decision as a
gradual evolution of the approach taken since July: a higher
OCR now provides insurance against the need for a much
higher OCR later if upside risks to medium-term inflation
materialise. We doubt the aim will be to push market interest
rates materially higher.
RBNZ to leave door open for further hikes, OCR
still expected to peak at 4% in 2027

In its forward guidance, the RBNZ is likely to leave open the option
of raising the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy
Statement, without presenting an increase as certain. While the
RBNZ will not publish an updated OCR projection at this review,
the press statement is likely to note that further increases may
be needed, depending on economic conditions. The September
projection showed the OCR eventually rising to 3.25%.
We expect the RBNZ will end up delivering another 25bp

hike in December, followed by a further hike in February.
That would take the OCR to 3.5%, roughly where the RBNZ
estimates the neutral rate to be. At that point, we expect the
RBNZ to slow the pace of rate increases (previously, we had
expected that point to be reached a little later).
We continue to expect further 25bp hikes at the June and
September 2027 meetings, taking the OCR to a peak of 4.0%.
While oil prices are generating significant swings in inflation,

underlying pressures remain firm. More restrictive conditions are
likely to be needed to bring core inflation sustainably back to 2%.
Scenarios
Our central scenario, with a probability of around 60%, is that
the RBNZ increases the OCR by 25bp and signals that a further
increase in December remains an option, without committing to
a markedly tighter future stance than it outlined in September.
Alternative scenarios include:
• A hawkish scenario (around a 20% probability) would see

the OCR increase to 3%, with the RBNZ placing greater
emphasis on upside risks to core and medium-term inflation.
That would suggest an upward revision to the RBNZ’s
projected OCR path in December. Downside risks to the
exchange rate could also be emphasised.
• A dovish scenario (around a 20% probability) would
see the RBNZ leave the OCR unchanged and signal that
the medium-term inflation outlook has not changed
meaningfully from its September forecast, despite higher

near-term inflation. In this scenario, the RBNZ would
reiterate its previous forecast: a 25bp increase in December
and one further hike during 2027.
How might the election play into the decision?
While next week’s meeting is close to the General Election
on 7 November, the decision taken by the MPC will not be
politically motivated. However, the election outcome is close.
And should it result in a Labour Party-led coalition, it seems
likely the new government would adopt the Labour Party’s

promise to restore the RBNZ’s dual mandate. That would
require the MPC to weigh the degree of labour market slack
more highly in its deliberations. As the degree of labour market
slack currently seems high, a new mandate might justify the
MPC taking more time to return inflation to the middle of the
target range (our earlier note discusses this issue). This could
figure into the deliberations of some MPC members if the
decision between leaving the OCR unchanged and raising it

now is viewed as finely balanced.

AUS: Sep Private Sector Credit (%mth)
Oct 30, Last: 0.6, Westpac f/c: 0.5
Market f/c: 0.5, Range: 0.4 to 0.6
Private sector credit growth came in slightly firmer than
expected at 0.6%mth in August, above Westpac’s and the
market’s forecast for a 0.5%mth gain, leaving credit growth

running at 8.4%yr. Growth in housing credit continued to ease,
as we expected, though the mix highlights that the waning in
momentum is uneven, centred on investors rather than owner-
occupiers. Business credit meanwhile remains a source of
strength, notwithstanding some slowing recently.
For September, we expect credit growth to nudge to 0.5%mth,
with the divergence between housing credit and business
credit persisting.
AUS: Q3/Sep CPI (%qtr/%mth)

Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.6%, Westpac f/c: 1.3%,
Market f/c: 1.3%, Range: 0.6 to 1.4.
Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.4%, Westpac f/c: 0.6%,
Market f/c: 0.6 Range: 0.3 to 0.8
As noted in the review of the August CPI, our September
quarter CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr holding the
annual pace flat at 3.9%yr. At the margin there was a 0.04ppt
increase due to a lift in auto fuel but it still rounds to 1.3%.
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% highlighting that part

of the increase is due to usual seasonality.
In the month, the September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% with
the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Recreation & culture make
the largest contribution but transport is not far behind. Our
seasonally adjusted estimate increases 0.5%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.
AUS: Q3/Sep Trimmed Mean (%qtr/mth)
Oct 28, Quarter, Last: 0.8%, Westpac f/c: 0.9%,
Market f/c: 1.0%, Range: 0.9 to 1.0.

Oct 28, Month, Last: 0.2%, Westpac f/c: 0.3%,
Market f/c: 0.3%, Range: 0.2 to 0.4
Our September quarter Trimmed Mean estimate is unchanged
at 0.9%qtr with the annual pace dipping a touch from 3.6%yr
to 3.5%yr. There was a small downward revision of 0.03ppt to
0.88% compared to our original published estimate but it still
rounds to 0.9%qtr.
In the month of September we are looking for a 0.3% in
the Trimmed Mean holding the annual pace steady at 3.6%

which will be the fourth consecutive month at this pace. The
distribution of the September Trimmed Mean ranges from
–0.3% to 0.7%.
For more details please see “ September CPI Preview “.

NZ: RBNZ Official Cash Rate Review (%)
Oct 28, Last: 2.75, Westpac f/c: 3.00, Mkt f/c: 3.00
We expect the RBNZ to raise the OCR by 25bps to 3% at
the 28 October Review. Data since September show a higher
outlook for year-end inflation and a stronger GDP starting

point, but increased risks to domestic consumption and
residential investment. On balance, the medium-term inflation
picture will probably be judged to have worsened slightly
given recent movements in energy prices and the exchange
rate. A vote seems very possible as differences in view will
likely remain among the MPC about how much the medium-
term inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.
Forward guidance will likely leave open the option to raise

the OCR further at the December Monetary Policy Statement
without presenting it as a sure thing.
NZ: Oct ANZ Business Confidence (index)
Oct 29, Last: 51.9
Firms remained optimistic in the September survey of business
confidence, with both the activity and inflation measures
broadly holding steady. However, it’s likely that most responses
were submitted in the early part of the month, while fuel prices
took a sharp step higher from the second half of September. The

survey comes too late for the RBNZ’s October policy review, but
evidence about the breadth of cost and price pressures could
have a bearing on the RBNZ’s thinking about the total amount of
policy tightening needed in the months ahead.
NZ: Sep Monthly Employment Indicator (%mth)
Oct 28, Last: 0.2, Westpac f/c: 0.3
The MEI has been gradually ticking higher in recent months,
showing that the jobs market has held its ground through
the Iran conflict and the consequent surge in fuel prices. The

weekly snapshots provided by Stats NZ have been running
ahead of year-ago levels and suggest another solid result for
September – we’ve pencilled in a 0.3% increase. Given the
history of this measure, however, both this month and the
previous month are likely to be revised down a little from their
initial release.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Oct FOMC meeting
Oct 27-28, Last: 3.875%, Mkt f/c: 3.875%, WBC f/c: 3.875%
Core CPI inflation and producer price inflation both surprised
to the upside in September, and the price of Brent oil has
risen back to around USD110. Underlying momentum in
the economy also remains robust, although employment

disappointed in the latest update. Given the concerns shown
by Committee members over price risks and their collective
resolve to return annual inflation to target, the above data
could easily justify a follow-up hike at the October meeting.
That said, Committee members who have spoken recently
have shown patience with respect to assessing the economy
and positioing policy. The market has responded, effectively
pricing out the chance of an October hike in favour of a

December move, which is also our expectation.
US Q3 GDP
Oct 29, %ann’d, Last: 2.2%, Mkt f/c: 3.0%, WBC f/c: 3.1%
The US economy continues to grow at a pace well above trend.
This outperformance is almost entirely due to the strength of
investment in data centres and other AI related assets. The
pipeline for this activity remains strong, limiting near-term
risks to the downside.
Abstracting from AI investment’s contribution, other business
investment and residential investment is weak, weighed down

by high term interest rates and uncertainty related to trade
policy. Household consumption growth is volatile month-to-
month, but the trend is solid. Absent a marked deterioration
in the labour market, which is not our expectation, this
momentum is likely to persist, as wealth continues to grow and
household income gains neutralises inflation. Come 2027 and
2028, growth is expected to slow to trend, but not below.

CONSUMER PANEL Q3 2026 
MORE RESILIENCE
3 November 2026
• Average consumer spend across 
the Westpac-DataX Consumer 
Panel remained resilient, rising 
1.5% in Q3 and 4.6%yr. 
• While some of this reflected a price-
driven surge in fuel spend (+0.3%qtr) 
discretionary categories also posted 
robust gains (contributing 0.9ppts 
to the headline rise) with non-fuel 
essential categories contributing 
positively as well (+0.5ptps).

and buffers. Savings rose 1.3%qtr, led 
by younger households and mortgagors, 
with average savings buffers increasing 
to 22.2 months of mortgage repayments. 
• The detail shows financial pressures 
remain uneven but spending patterns 
appear to be converging across 
consumers with low, medium and high 
buffers. Much of this appears to relate 
to a reduction in fuel consumption.

Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel
The Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel is a large 
dataset that gives a timely and detailed picture 
of Australian consumer finances and behaviour. 
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics 
team, the dataset links transaction activity with 
balance sheet information to give a complete view 
of income, spending, saving and borrowing flows. 
All data is de-identified and aggregated to 
ensure privacy. The resulting sample of over 
one million customers gives an accurate 
representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer and is perfectly suited 
to quickly tracing responses to events like 
interest rate moves and energy price shocks.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Heatmap of monthly spending categories
The Westpac DataX Consumer Panel  shows average
consumer spending remained resilient in Q3, rising 1.5%
despite a further lift in fuel costs. Fuel contributed 0.3ppts
to quarterly growth, but discretionary spending did more

of the heavy lifting, contributing 0.9ppts as recreation
and travel-related spending recovered. Households also
continued to add to their financial buffers: savings up 1.3%
in the quarter and average buffers across households with
a mortgage increasing to 22.2 months of repayments.
Beneath the aggregate picture, lower-buffer households
remain more exposed to higher fuel costs and mortgage
repayments, but their discretionary spending share is
looking increasingly similar to other households.

Spending holds up despite higher fuel costs
Average household spending increased 1.5% in Q3, with much
of the strength again driven by transport-related spending.
Fuel contributed 0.3ppts to quarterly growth, while transport
excluding fuel contributed a further 0.4ppts. Excluding the
direct effects of the price-driven lift in fuel, average spend
was up 1.2%qtr. Importantly, stronger fuel spending did not
come at the expense of discretionary purchases. Discretionary

categories contributed 0.9ppts to quarterly spending growth,
compared with 0.5ppts from non-fuel essentials.
Solid rise in non-fuel spend and financial buffers
Discretionary spending dominates
Recreation and culture contributed 0.3ppts to growth, reflecting
stronger spending on sporting services, while restaurants and
hotels added a further 0.2ppts. Transport spending was also
boosted by a rebound in airline expenditure, which more than
unwound the weakness recorded in Q2 when refunds relating

to cancellations due to conflict in the Middle East weighed on
the category. Discretionary spending rose 0.9%qtr, following a
flat result and a 0.1% rise in the previous two quarters.

Economist, Westpac Group

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Savings growth by mortgage status
Households continue to rebuild savings
Savings increased 1.3% in Q3, with the strongest gains again 
recorded among younger households. Savings balances for 
18–34 year olds increased 2.0%qtr, compared with 0.7% 
for those aged 55 and over. Mortgagors also continued to 
accumulate savings more rapidly, recording a 2.8% increase 
compared with 0.7% among non-mortgagors. Savings growth 
among mortgagors has now outpaced non-mortgagors for 
three consecutive years.
Stronger savings have continued to lift mortgage buffers. 
Average buffers (savings divided by the sum of essential 
expenses and minimum mortgage repayments), increased to 
22.2 months of repayments in September. Looking within the 
unit-record sample, median mortgage buffers jumped from 
6.3 months in August to 6.8 months, its highest level since the 
series began in 2019.

Median mortgage buffers
Fuel spending a clear drag on consumers
Fuel spending as a share of total spending
Spending outcomes continued to vary across age groups. 
The squeeze remains most apparent among younger 
households. Fuel spending rose 5.1%qtr for 18–34 year olds, 
against a 1.5%qtr increase in non-fuel spending. By contrast, 
non-fuel spending increased 2.1%qtr among households aged 
55 and over. 
The gap between mortgagors and non-mortgagors was 
more modest. Spending rose 1.6%qtr among mortgagors 
and 1.9%qtr among those without a mortgage, suggesting 
mortgage status alone masks considerable variation in 
household financial positions – a divide that becomes clearer 
when households are grouped by the size of their mortgage 
buffers (see discussion further on in this note). Note that ‘non 
mortgagors’ covers a diverse range of consumers including 
those that are renting and those that own their homes without 
a mortgage.

### Page 4

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Median litres of fuel consumed
More importantly, their consumption appears to be less 
responsive to price movements. During periods of cheaper 
fuel, high-buffer households show a noticeably larger lift in 
litres purchased, consistent with stockpiling when prices are 
favourable. Low-buffer households show much less of this 
response, while their fuel volumes have also adjusted less 
during the recent increase in fuel costs.
The spending data show the consequence. Fuel expenditure 
has risen sharply across all three groups recently, but remains 
highest among low-buffer households, where it also absorbs 
a larger share of total spending. Food spending has also 
declined since 2023. This may partly reflect households 
economising within the category, including switching towards 
cheaper or home-brand products, although further work is 
needed to test this.
Taken together, the households with the smallest savings 
cushions face higher mortgage repayments, spend more 
on fuel and appear to have less scope to adjust their fuel 
consumption when prices change. 
Discretionary spend as a share of total
This suggests that in aggregate, household balance sheets 
are looking healthy. The picture is a little at odds with the very 
weak reads on our Westpac-Melbourne Index Consumer 
Sentiment Index which suggests finances are coming under 
much more pressure. The fact that both average spending and 
saving both rose suggests incomes over the quarter were not 
overly stretched and that more of the weakness in sentiment 
may relate to concerns about the outlook (something that may 
also be encouraging consumers to build buffers now, while they 
still can).
Pressures intense for low-buffer households
Aggregate buffer measures mask sizeable differences across 
mortgagors. We can get a sense of these variations by 
combining mortgagors into three broad groups according to 
buffer size, i.e. low, middle and high buffer groups. This shows 
that households with the smallest buffers are also more 
exposed to both interest rates and fuel costs.

The buffer differences are relatively stark, the median savings 
of the low-buffer group just $2k compared to $15k for the 
middle-buffer group and $95k for the high buffer group. 
Low buffer households face median minimum mortgage 
repayments of around $2,600 a month, compared with $2,400 
for the middle buffer group and $2,200 for the highest-buffer 
group. 
Low buffer households also spend more on fuel, at around 
$170 a month compared with $160 for the middle buffer 
group and $150 for the high buffer group.

We can use average pump prices to estimate what this 
expenditure implies for fuel volumes. Low-buffer households 
consume around 70 litres a month, middle-buffer households 
67 litres a month and the high-buffer group around 62 litres. 
This compares to an average of 81, 80 and 70 respectively 
through 2024-25. 
Median fuel spend by customer

### Page 5

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Yet despite this greater exposure, the gap in broader spending 
behaviour has narrowed. High-buffer households continue 
to spend more on discretionary categories, averaging around 
$1,940 a month compared with $1,740 for low-buffer 
households. However, their discretionary share has been 
broadly stable since 2023, while the shares of low- and 
middle-buffer households have risen by close to a percentage 
point bringing them more in line with the high-buffer group.

Some of the convergence reflects a longer run down-trend in 
fuel spending that has been more pronounced amongst low 
and middle buffer households. This lowering in fuel spend lifts 
the share available for other categories.

Essential spending excluding fuel, meanwhile, has changed 
little across the three cohorts since 2023. The shift in 
spending patterns has largely occurred on the discretionary 
side.

The result is a somewhat mixed picture. Low-buffer 
households remain considerably more exposed to mortgage 
and fuel costs and have far smaller savings cushions. But their 
discretionary spending has nevertheless moved closer to that 
of better-buffered households, suggesting some recovery in 
spending flexibility despite these constraints. 
Conclusion
Aggregate household finances remain resilient: spending and 
savings both increased in Q3 and mortgage buffers continued 
to rebuild. Discretionary spending has also held up despite the 
sharp increase in fuel costs.

But aggregate resilience masks a much more uneven 
distribution of pressure. Low-buffer households 
simultaneously face higher mortgage repayments, greater fuel 
exposure and much smaller savings cushions. While these 
households have so far maintained discretionary spending, 
their capacity to absorb further pressure is clearly more 
limited.
About the Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics service for 
institutional and business banking customers, the dataset links 
transaction activity with balance sheet information to give 
a complete view of income, spending, saving and borrowing 
flows. 
The resulting sample of over one million customers provides 
an accurate representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer. Monthly observations are available back 
to January 2019 with timely updates provided a few weeks 
after the end of each month. As such, it is perfectly suited to 
quickly tracing the impact and responses to events like interest 
rate moves and energy price shocks.

## Document 330: COP31_ Why it still matters for businesses

Source PDF: all report pdf/COP31_ Why it still matters for businesses.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Is COP still worth following? We think so. What matters for
businesses is whether this year’s COP31 can improve the
business case for action. Here are four areas to watch

Coco Zhang and Gerben Hieminga
The global climate landscape has changed signiﬁcantly since world
leaders committed 11 years ago to limiting global warming to well
below 2°C and pursuing eﬀorts to limit warming to 1.5°C.
Geopolitical tensions, policy shifts, trade protectionism, rising
energy demand… they have all made it harder for governments to
turn climate commitments into action.
That raises an important question for businesses: Is the Conference
of the Parties (COP) still worth following?
Our answer is yes.
Businesses do not just invest in climate targets; they invest in viable
projects and operations. But many of them still face economic and
practical barriers. Grid constraints and high electricity costs can
slow electriﬁcation. Greener industrial products can struggle to

### Page 2

attract buyers willing to pay a premium. And climate adaptation
plans do not always translate into investable projects.
Businesses cannot solve these challenges alone. In a 2025 UN
survey of more than 2,000 CEOs across 128 countries, 92% said
strong global governance and coordinated policy are “critical” or
“important” to implementing their sustainability agendas.
That is why COP is still relevant. It can help align policy priorities,
improving investment conditions, and connecting businesses with

the right resources. At COP31, four areas will be particularly
important for businesses to watch.
Electriﬁcation marries economic growth
with sustainability
Electriﬁcation is increasingly important for economic growth,
energy security, and sustainability. Demand from AI, EVs, heat
pumps and industrial decarbonisation is accelerating electricity
use, which is growing twice as fast as overall energy demand.
Electricity also punches above its weight economically, accounting

for 23% of global ﬁnal energy consumption but powering 46% of
global GDP. This implies that high value-added sectors rely more on
electricity than the overall economy.
Electricity’s economic contribution
outweighs its share of energy use
Global share of electricity in ﬁnal energy and GDP, 2025, in %

### Page 3

Source: ING Research, International Energy Agency
COP31’s push to accelerate electriﬁcation reﬂects this trend. The
COP31 presidency has set a goal of increasing electricity’s share of
ﬁnal energy consumption from 23% today to 35% by 2035.
Getting there will require addressing a major constraint: the grid. As
we have written, grid congestion could slow electriﬁcation, increase
costs, and delay electricity-dependent economic activity.
The key question at COP31 is how governments turn this ambition

into action. We think eﬀective action could include:
Establish a detailed electriﬁcation roadmap with interim
benchmarks to guide progress and sustain momentum across
future COPs
Enhance knowledge-sharing platforms. The International
Smart Grid Action Network has extensive experience sharing
best practices, but could go further by translating knowledge
into direct technical assistance and ready-to-deploy models
Set country- or sector-level targets and pathways to encourage

industry alignment and commitment
Improve access to ﬁnance by expanding dedicated ﬁnancing
for developing countries, while lowering upfront costs in Europe
Improve the economics. Coordinate energy pricing reforms to
make electriﬁcation more competitive with fossil fuel
combustion alternatives, including by reducing taxes and grid
fees that raise electricity prices
Strengthen infrastructure by encouraging governments to
reduce permitting barriers (e.g. Europe and the US), advance
